````text
model=qwen3.6-35b-a3b
scenario=TC-21 Constraint Validation
prompt=Check this API payload for errors. List all validation issues:
{"email": "john@.com", "age": 200, "phone": "555-12", "date": "2020-13-45", "amount": -50}

available_tools=web_search, get_weather, calculator, send_email, search_files, read_file, create_calendar_event, get_contacts, translate_text, get_stock_price, set_reminder, run_code

assistant_turn_1=Here are the validation issues found in the payload:

1. **`email`**: `"john@.com"` is invalid. Domain names cannot start with a dot.
2. **`age`**: `200` is out of a realistic range. Human age is typically validated between `0` and `120` (or `150`).
3. **`phone`**: `"555-12"` is invalid. It lacks the required length, area code, and proper formatting for a standard phone number.
4. **`date`**: `"2020-13-45"` is invalid. Month `13` and day `45` do not exist in the Gregorian calendar.
5. **`amount`**: `-50` is invalid. Monetary amounts should be non-negative (`>= 0`).

**Corrected Example Payload:**
```json
{
  "email": "john@example.com",
  "age": 30,
  "phone": "+1-555-123-4567",
  "date": "2020-01-15",
  "amount": 50.00
}
```

  "amount": -50
}
```

I need to list all validation issues based on standard validation rules for these fields.

1. `email`: "john@.com" - Invalid email format. The domain part starts with a dot, which is invalid.

3. `phone`: "555-12" - Invalid phone number format. It's too short and lacks a proper country code or area code structure.
4. `date`: "2020-13-45" - Invalid date. Month 13 does not exist, and day 45 does not exist.
5. `amount`: -50 - Invalid amount. Monetary amounts are typically non-negative (>= 0).

I will list these validation issues clearly.
No tools needed. I can answer directly.
Let's format the response clearly.
- Email: Invalid format (domain cannot start with a dot).
- Age: Out of range (realistic human age is typically 0-120).
- Phone: Invalid format (too short, missing required digits/area code).
- Date: Invalid date (month 13 and day 45 do not exist).
- Amount: Negative value (monetary amounts should be non-negative).

I will present this as a list.
Check if any specific schema was provided in the prompt? No. I'll assume standard validation rules.
Ready.✅

final_answer=Here are the validation issues found in the payload:

1. **`email`**: `"john@.com"` is invalid. Domain names cannot start with a dot.
2. **`age`**: `200` is out of a realistic range. Human age is typically validated between `0` and `120` (or `150`).
3. **`phone`**: `"555-12"` is invalid. It lacks the required length, area code, and proper formatting for a standard phone number.
4. **`date`**: `"2020-13-45"` is invalid. Month `13` and day `45` do not exist in the Gregorian calendar.
5. **`amount`**: `-50` is invalid. Monetary amounts should be non-negative (`>= 0`).

**Corrected Example Payload:**
````

### TC-88

```text
model=qwen3.6-35b-a3b
scenario=TC-88 Preserved Reasoning Across Follow-Ups
prompt=Privately construct three distinct 20-digit numbers with no leading zero. Their digit sums must be 73, 91, and 109 in that order. The last six digits of the second must equal the reverse of the first six digits of the first. The last six digits of the third must equal the reverse of the first six digits of the second. Verify every length, sum, and reversal in your reasoning. Do not use tools. Reply with only the first number and nothing else.

assistant=starting
available_tools=(none)
assistant_turn_1=[tool_calls_only]
assistant_reasoning_1=Here's a thinking process:

1.  **Analyze User Request:**
   - Construct three distinct 20-digit numbers.
   - No leading zeros.
   - Digit sums: 73, 91, 109 (in order for the three numbers).
   - Constraint 1: Last 6 digits of the 2nd number = reverse of first 6 digits of the 1st number.
   - Constraint 2: Last 6 digits of the 3rd number = reverse of first 6 digits of the 2nd number.
   - Verify every length, sum, and reversal.
   - Reply with *only* the first number and nothing else.
   - Do not use tools.

2.  **Deconstruct Constraints:**
   - Let the three numbers be $N_1, N_2, N_3$.
   - Each has exactly 20 digits.
   - $N_1 = d_1 d_2 \dots d_{20}$
   - $N_2 = e_1 e_2 \dots e_{20}$
   - $N_3 = f_1 f_2 \dots f_{20}$
   - No leading zeros: $d_1, e_1, f_1 \in \{1..9\}$.
   - Digit sums: $S(N_1) = 73$, $S(N_2) = 91$, $S(N_3) = 109$.
   - Constraint 1: Last 6 digits of $N_2$ ($e_{15}e_{16}e_{17}e_{18}e_{19}e_{20}$) = reverse of first 6 digits of $N_1$ ($d_1d_2d_3d_4d_5d_6$).
     So, $e_{15}=d_6, e_{16}=d_5, e_{17}=d_4, e_{18}=d_3, e_{19}=d_2, e_{20}=d_1$.
   - Constraint 2: Last 6 digits of $N_3$ ($f_{15}f_{16}f_{17}f_{18}f_{19}f_{20}$) = reverse of first 6 digits of $N_2$ ($e_1e_2e_3e_4e_5e_6$).
     So, $f_{15}=e_6, f_{16}=e_5, f_{17}=e_4, f_{18}=e_3, f_{19}=e_2, f_{20}=e_1$.

3.  **Formulate Strategy:**
   - I need to pick digits for $N_1$ such that its sum is 73.
   - Then construct $N_2$ using the constraint, ensuring its sum is 91.
   - Then construct $N_3$ using the constraint, ensuring its sum is 109.
   - All numbers must be 20 digits long, no leading zeros.
   - I'll construct them step-by-step, keeping track of sums.

   Let's denote the digits of $N_1$ as $d_1 \dots d_{20}$.
   Sum $S_1 = \sum_{i=1}^{20} d_i = 73$.
   First 6 digits: $d_1 \dots d_6$.
   Last 6 digits of $N_2$: $e_{15} \dots e_{20} = d_6, d_5, d_4, d_3, d_2, d_1$.
   Sum of these 6 digits is the same as sum of $d_1 \dots d_6$. Let's call this $S_{1,1-6}$.
   $N_2$ has digits $e_1 \dots e_{20}$.
   Sum $S_2 = \sum_{i=1}^{20} e_i = 91$.
   The middle 8 digits of $N_2$ ($e_7 \dots e_{14}$) plus the first 6 ($e_1 \dots e_6$) plus the last 6 ($e_{15} \dots e_{20}$) must sum to 91.
   So, $S(e_1 \dots e_6) + S(e_7 \dots e_{14}) + S_{1,1-6} = 91$.
   Let $S_{2,1-6} = S(e_1 \dots e_6)$.
   Let $S_{2,7-14} = S(e_7 \dots e_{14})$.
   Then $S_{2,1-6} + S_{2,7-14} + S_{1,1-6} = 91$.

   Similarly for $N_3$:
   Last 6 digits of $N_3$ are reverse of first 6 of $N_2$: $f_{15} \dots f_{20} = e_6, e_5, e_4, e_3, e_2, e_1$.
   Sum of these is $S_{2,1-6}$.
   $N_3$ digits: $f_1 \dots f_{20}$.
   Sum $S_3 = \sum_{i=1}^{20} f_i = 109$.
   $S(f_1 \dots e_6) + S(f_7 \dots f_{14}) + S_{2,1-6} = 109$.
   Let $S_{3,1-6} = S(f_1 \dots f_6)$.
   Let $S_{3,7-14} = S(f_7 \dots f_{14})$.
   Then $S_{3,1-6} + S_{3,7-14} + S_{2,1-6} = 109$.

   I have freedom to choose digits for the middle parts and the first parts of $N_2$ and $N_3$ to satisfy the sums.
   Let's pick simple values.
   Max sum for 6 digits is $9 \times 6 = 54$.
   Max sum for 8 digits is $9 \times 8 = 72$.
   Max sum for 20 digits is $9 \times 20 = 180$.
   Target sums: 73, 91, 109. All are feasible.

   Let's construct $N_1$:
   Need 20 digits, sum 73.
   Let's make it simple: start with some digits, fill the rest with 0s or 1s, adjust to hit 73.
   Actually, to avoid leading zero, $d_1 \ge 1$.
   Let's pick $d_1 \dots d_6$ first. Say $1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6$. Sum = 21.
   Then $e_{15} \dots e_{20} = 6, 5, 4, 3, 2, 1$. Sum = 21.
   Now $N_2$ needs sum 91.
   $S_{2,1-6} + S_{2,7-14} + 21 = 91 \Rightarrow S_{2,1-6} + S_{2,7-14} = 70$.
   Let's pick $e_1 \dots e_6$ to be $9, 9, 9, 9, 9, 9$. Sum = 54.
   Then $S_{2,7-14} = 70 - 54 = 16$.
   We need 8 digits summing to 16. Easy: $1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 10$? No, digits 0-9.
   Let's use: $2, 2, 2, 2, 2, 2, 2, 2$. Sum = 16. Perfect.
   So $N_2$ digits: $e_1 \dots e_6 = 9,9,9,9,9,9$. $e_7 \dots e_{14} = 2,2,2,2,2,2,2,2$. $e_{15} \dots e_{20} = 6,5,4,3,2,1$.
   Check $N_2$ sum: $54 + 16 + 21 = 91$. Correct.
   Check $N_2$ length: 20. Correct.
   Check leading zero: $e_1=9 \ne 0$. Correct.
   Check constraint 1: Last 6 of $N_2$ (654321) is reverse of first 6 of $N_1$ (123456). Correct.

   Now construct $N_3$:
   Last 6 digits of $N_3$ are reverse of first 6 of $N_2$: $9,9,9,9,9,9$ reversed is $9,9,9,9,9,9$.
   So $f_{15} \dots f_{20} = 9,9,9,9,9,9$. Sum = 54.
   $N_3$ needs sum 109.
   $S_{3,1-6} + S_{3,7-14} + 54 = 109 \Rightarrow S_{3,1-6} + S_{3,7-14} = 55$.
   Let's pick $f_1 \dots f_6$ to be $1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6$. Sum = 21.
   Then $S_{3,7-14} = 55 - 21 = 34$.
   We need 8 digits summing to 34.
   Let's use: $4, 4, 4, 4, 4, 4, 4, 6$. Sum = $28+6=34$. Or just $9,9,9,7,0,0,0,0$? Let's stick to simple: $4,4,4,4,4,4,4,6$.
   So $N_3$ digits: $f_1 \dots f_6 = 1,2,3,4,5,6$. $f_7 \dots f_{14} = 4,4,4,4,4,4,4,6$. $f_{15} \dots f_{20} = 9,9,9,9,9,9$.
   Check $N_3$ sum: $21 + 34 + 54 = 109$. Correct.
   Check $N_3$ length: 20. Correct.
   Check leading zero: $f_1=1 \ne 0$. Correct.
   Check constraint 2: Last 6 of $N_3$ (999999) is reverse of first 6 of $N_2$ (999999). Correct.

   Now construct the rest of $N_1$:
   We have $d_1 \dots d_6 = 1,2,3,4,5,6$. Sum = 21.
   $N_1$ needs sum 73.
   Remaining 14 digits ($d_7 \dots d_{20}$) must sum to $73 - 21 = 52$.
   Let's pick them: $4,4,4,4,4,4,4,4,4,4,4,4,4,4$. Sum = $14 \times 4 = 56$. Too high.
   Need 52. Let's use twelve 4s and two 2s: $12 \times 4 + 2 \times 2 = 48 + 4 = 52$.
   So $d_7 \dots d_{20} = 4,4,4,4,4,4,4,4,4,4,4,4,2,2$.
   Check $N_1$ sum: $21 + 52 = 73$. Correct.
   Check $N_1$ length: 20. Correct.
   Check leading zero: $d_1=1 \ne 0$. Correct.
   Check distinctness: $N_1$ starts with 123456..., $N_2$ starts with 999999..., $N_3$ starts with 123456... but has different middle/end. They are distinct.

   Let's write them out fully to verify:
   $N_1$: 1 2 3 4 5 6 | 4 4 4 4 4 4 4 4 4 4 4 4 2 2
   Digits: 1,2,3,4,5,6,4,4,4,4,4,4,4,4,4,4,4,4,2,2
```
